No key patterns are needed since none of those commands take keys, which is why `FLUSHALL` and all data access are denied.\n\n**Verified**\n- `app` SET `cache:probe`=`ok` on 6379, GET returns `ok` on 6380; a second live key propagated in under a second.\n- `AUTH default` → `WRONGPASS` on both ports; `AUTH repl repl-secret` → `OK`, but `FLUSHALL` → `NOPERM`.\n- Plaintext: `redis-cli` without `--tls` gets `Connection reset by peer`; a raw Python socket sending `PING\\r\\n` gets `ConnectionResetError` with zero bytes read — no RESP spoken. TLSv1.3 handshakes on both ports verify `OK` against the CA.\n- `master_link_status:up`, `connected_slaves:1`, `state=online`, replica read-only (`READONLY` on write).\n\n**`/results/repl.json`** — generated from live `INFO replication` output, not hardcoded; UTF-8 with trailing newline:\n\n```json\n{\"master_tls_port\": 6379, \"replica_tls_port\": 6380, \"role_master\": \"master\",\n \"role_replica\": \"slave\", \"master_link_status\": \"up\", \"tls_build\": \"yes\"}\n```\n\nTwo things worth knowing: the server cert has CN=`redis` with no SAN, which works here only because Redis 7.2's `tls.c` verifies the chain but never the hostname — if you later front this with a proxy or client that does enforce hostname matching, you'll need a cert with a proper SAN. And `tls-auth-clients no` means any process that can reach loopback may attempt AUTH; the ACL passwords are the only barrier, so that setup depends on the host being trusted.","ttft_ms":2969,"type":"result","duration_ms":280855,"uuid":"677ee7fd-f73b-47ec-bcad-c74a92aba47f","ttft_stream_ms":2009,"time_to_request_ms":61,"first_content_frame_ms":2161,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
